# Configuration knob removal inventory

Started from the planning snapshot of 2026-10-26. This preserves the work so
removal can resume after worktree integration. The removals that were actually
applied are listed in [Removal log](#removal-log); everything else remains a
proposal unless called out in the 2026-10-28 refresh below.

## Integration status

The settings resolver and subsequent full-graph cleanup are integrated in the
active checkout. The current scan uses `/home/ray/Projects/ZeroMoE/.worktrees/ling-env-cleanup`, branch
`codex/ling-env-cleanup`, HEAD `0938cd4b36a01a2e0420b187043af4c600b8814c`, plus its working-tree changes. The production inventory reports
111 environment names, 107 CLI settings,
41 graph settings, 232 TOML settings,
7 compile-time macro names, 4 build-cache inputs,
and 1 ZeroMoE-specific CMake option. A separate
`test_environment` category lists 17 test-only selectors and fixture inputs; those are not runtime settings.
The companion JSON's `current_inventory` contains exact names and source locations.
Its older `controls` array and most detailed per-control rows retain
the four-worktree historical assessment; treat those recommendations as
candidates until checked against current readers and behavior. New dispositions
should update `review_decision` and `removal_status` in the JSON.

Record new decisions in the JSON `review_decision` field rather than editing
generated historical counts by hand.

## Removal log

Scope for removal decisions: simplify environment and build-time controls after
checking their consumers. Remove dead controls only with reachability evidence;
remove test or experiment controls when the normal validated behavior is clear
and the experiment has no supported product use. Each entry names the evidence
and behavior affected. Canonical TOML/CLI keys are removed only when they expose
the same retired behavior, as with cuFile and the experimental GPU-assist
override.

### 2026-10-27 — full-graph gates and Qwen preload defaults

| Control | Surface | Evidence | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ZEROMOE_QWEN4EXP_FULL_GRAPH`, `ZEROMOE_QWEN4EXP_FULL_GRAPH_DECODE` (K0372, K0373) | environment | `env_enabled()`/`env_decode_enabled()` were the only readers. The engine config already defaults both TOML keys to true, so removing the env reads preserves every default run. | Deleted both readers and their declarations; `g_config_full_graph`/`g_config_full_graph_decode` default true and are set only by `qwen4exp_full_graph_configure()`. TOML `qwen4exp_full_graph=false` remains the explicit fallback/diagnostic. |
| `ZEROMOE_GLM_DSA_FULL_GRAPH`, `_PREFILL` (K0294, K0295) | environment | `GlmDsaGraphModel::enabled()` read these per phase. Nothing else consumed them; MTP already returned false before the env check. | `enabled()` now returns `!engine.mtp_enabled` for both phases. MTP remains the documented opt-out. |
| `ZEROMOE_LING_FULL_GRAPH` (K0302) | environment | `ling_full_graph_enabled()` and the native-graph conjunction were the only readers. Native/hybrid selection is a separate implementation choice. | `ling_full_graph_enabled()` returns true under CUDA; `bailingmoe3_native_graph_enabled()` now reads only `ZEROMOE_LING_NATIVE_GRAPH`. |
| `ZEROMOE_DEEPSEEK_V41_FULL_GRAPH` (K0112) | environment | `v41_env_enabled()` guarded prefill prepare/run and the top-level enabled query. The decode query fell back to this name when its own override was unset. | `v41_full_graph_enabled()` returns true. The decode fallback now defaults to true; `ZEROMOE_DEEPSEEK_V41_FULL_GRAPH_DECODE=0` remains an explicit native-decode comparison. |
| `ZEROMOE_QWEN4EXP_GRAPH_PRELOAD_LAYERS` (K0386) | environment | Legacy alias for `graph_device_expert_layers`. Default 0 made the whole-layer block absent unless a script set it. | Default 2. `0` remains the explicit no-whole-layer/global-pool sentinel. An enabled block must have at least 2 layers: env `1` is raised to 2 with a warning, TOML `1` is rejected. |
| `ZEROMOE_QWEN4EXP_GRAPH_PRELOAD_TRAILING` (K0388) | environment | Legacy alias for `graph_device_expert_trailing`. The code default was already false; documentation incorrectly said 1. | Default false (leading block). The flag remains an explicit placement selector matching llama.cpp `-ncmoe` when true, and has no effect when the layer count is 0. |

Verification for this batch:

- `graph_settings_test` passes against the updated defaults snapshot
  (`device_expert_layers=2`), including rejection of TOML `1` and preservation
  of the explicit `0` sentinel.
- A CUDA `zeromoe` build links; `build_provenance.py check` reports the current
  source commit and a clean manifest.
- CPU-only configure is unchanged (the Qwen/Ling/DeepSeek graph stubs still
  return false, because the graph needs the CUDA backend).
- Remaining references to the removed names in dated benchmark records
  (`bench/results/`, `docs/perf-record-*`, `configs/bench/perf-baselines.md`)
  are historical provenance, not live configuration; active scripts and
  current guides were updated.

### 2026-10-27 — settings family scoping (no env names removed)

This pass changes how resolved settings are attributed, not the inventory. Every
`ZEROMOE_*` name stays valid, so no JSON row changes status.

| Change | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `Family::GlmGraph` → `Family::Glm` | The settings enum now names concrete model families. GLM-5.x and GLM-DSA use the Glm row; the shared graph implementation is no longer a family. |
| `ZEROMOE_LING_*` aliases scoped to Ling | A Glm run no longer reads Ling's names, and a Ling run inherits `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_*` one way (Ling spelling first). Ling also inherits Glm's `gpu_assist_slots=3` family default. |
| `Field::claims` | TOML capability is declared per field instead of inferred from alias scope. Native Ling mode does not claim the GLM offload knobs; shared-graph Ling mode lists the Glm adapter. |

Verification for this pass:

- `zeromoe_cpu`, `graph_settings_test` and `graph_settings_binding_test` build
  with `-DZEROMOE_CPU_ONLY=ON -DZEROMOE_USE_GGML=ON`.
- `ctest -R '^graph_settings'` passes 2/2; the resolver tests cover Ling/Glm
  inheritance, reverse-inheritance rejection, TOML capability, and the
  `claims != 0` / `claims implies reads` invariants.
- The six touched CUDA-only translation units were host-syntax-checked with the
  CUDA include path and `-DZEROMOE_USE_GGML=1 -DZEROMOE_USE_GGML_CUDA=1`.
- `python3 tools/config_inventory.py --check` passes; all inventory rows keep
  their previous status because no environment name was added or removed.

### 2026-10-27 — retire the negative-skip opt-outs

`ZEROMOE_MMID_NO_NEGATIVE_SKIP` and `ZEROMOE_MMID_NO_BATCHED_NEGATIVE_SKIP`
looked like user settings but were controls for validating a ggml patch. The
repository pins ggml and applies `patches/ggml` on every build; `CMakeLists.txt`
calls `zeromoe_apply_ggml_patches()` and `scripts/build_ggml.sh` aborts when the
negative-skip marker is missing. A supported build therefore always has the
capability, and a stock ggml is not a supported configuration.

| Control | Surface | Evidence | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ZEROMOE_MMID_NO_NEGATIVE_SKIP` (K0343) | environment | The 2026-10-03 validation used `=1` to compare negative skipping with dummy-route masking; both produced byte-identical full logit dumps (`docs/option-b-global-pool-validation-2026-10-03.md`). The bundled ggml is always patched, so the remaining effect was to make a supported build silently take the slower dummy path. | Deleted the environment read. A stock ggml link still compiles (weak symbol) but warns once and uses dummy routes; the test `optout` mode and the bench `--no-negative-skip` flag were removed. |
| `ZEROMOE_MMID_NO_BATCHED_NEGATIVE_SKIP` (K0342) | environment | Patch `0006-ggml-cuda-mmq-id-inactive-slots` is mandatory for batched MMQ: the fallback shares dummy id 0, which breaks ggml's batched id compaction. No document, config or harness set the opt-out outside the patch A/B. | Deleted the environment read; absence of the batched symbol now warns once and falls back to dummy routes for unsupported builds. |

Verification for this pass:

- `test_moe_partial_route` passes in `capable` and `stock` modes; `stock` now
  exercises the warning path instead of an environment opt-out.
- `zeromoe_cpu` builds, `ctest -R '^graph_settings'` passes 2/2 and
  `python3 tools/config_inventory.py --check` passes.
- `python3 -m py_compile bench/option_b_global_pool_check.py` passes.
- `grep -rn 'MMID_NO_NEGATIVE_SKIP\|MMID_NO_BATCHED_NEGATIVE_SKIP'` finds only
  the dated validation note and the audit records that mark them removed.

### 2026-10-27 — environment reads routed through graphcfg (no names removed)

This pass removed direct `std::getenv` calls, not configuration names. Every
production `ZEROMOE_*` read in `memory/`, `backends/`, `core/` and `models/`
now goes through `graphcfg::env()` or a typed reader, so `log_effective()` can
report which knobs a run actually consulted. The two CUDA kernel files
(`backends/exl3/exl3_upstream_ops.cu` and
`kernels/quant/kquant_matmul.cu`) use the same reader. `graphcfg::atol_or()`
was added for the legacy `atol` call site in the memory guard.

The following raw reads are deliberate and are not migration gaps:

| Location | Variable | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `core/main.cpp`, `core/engine_runner_api.cpp`, `backends/cpu/cpu_main.cpp` | `ZEROMOE_API_KEY` | Secret; must not enter the logged settings surface. |
| `backends/exl3/exl3_decode.cpp` | `OMP_WAIT_POLICY`, `GOMP_SPINCOUNT` | Owned by the OpenMP runtime, not by ZeroMoE. |
| `core/qwen4exp_graph_mtp.cpp`, `core/mtp_*` / `models/**/mtp_*` | MTP knobs | Active MTP work; deliberately out of scope. |
| `patches/ggml*` | GGML-patch knobs | Bundled ggml C sources; they must not depend on a ZeroMoE header. |
| `bench/` (16 call sites) | Benchmark knobs | Developer harnesses, not production paths. `config_inventory.py` does not scan them. |

Verification for this pass:

- `zeromoe_cpu` builds; `ctest -R '^graph_settings'` passes 2/2.
- Host syntax checks pass for every touched `core/`, `models/` and backend TU;
  the two CUDA files compile with `nvcc -std=c++17 -arch=sm_80`.
- `test_moe_partial_route` passes in `capable` and `stock` modes.
- `python3 tools/config_inventory.py --check` passes.

### 2026-10-27 — remove the always-on ggml and server build macros

The previous pass made `ZEROMOE_USE_GGML` and `ZEROMOE_BUILD_SERVER` default to
`ON` but still advertised them as switches. Neither had a working `OFF` path, so
keeping them only invited configure-time changes the tree cannot honour. Both
capabilities are now unconditional and the option names are gone.

| Control | Surface | Evidence | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ZEROMOE_USE_GGML` (K0026, K0034, K1147) | CMake option, compile definition, 33 source guards | `OFF` never worked: `backends/cpu/kernels_cpu.cpp` needs `ggml_vec_dot_q2_0_q8_0`, which only ggml provides, so a no-ggml CPU build fails to compile. The previous pass made configure reject `OFF` for the same reason. | Deleted the option, its compile definitions, every `#if defined(ZEROMOE_USE_GGML)` guard and the `#else` stubs those guards protected (`kernels_cpu.cpp`, `kernels_cpu_neon.cpp`, `cpu_forward.cpp`, `cpu_engine.cpp`, `cpu_qwen_moe.hpp`, `cpu_qwen4exp.cpp`, `ggml_qwen_cpu_moe.cpp`, `ggml_resource_guards.hpp`, `main.cpp`, `engine_runner.hpp`). The ggml fetch/link block is unconditional. |
| `ZEROMOE_BUILD_SERVER` (K0017, K0029) | CMake option, compile definition, 7 source guards | `OFF` only compiled out `--serve`, `EngineRunner::run_api()` and the CPU API entry point; no supported build needs a binary without them, and the server tests are already gated by `ZEROMOE_SERVER_TESTS`. | Deleted the option, its compile definitions and every guard in `core/main.cpp`, `core/engine_runner_api.cpp` and `backends/cpu/cpu_main.cpp`. `add_subdirectory(server)` and the `zeromoe_cpu_api` link are unconditional. |
| `ZEROMOE_USE_LLAMA_CPP` (K0028, K1148) | CMake option | It only forced `ZEROMOE_USE_GGML=ON` plus a deprecation warning; it set no cache entry, path or definition of its own. With ggml unconditional the alias had nothing left to do. | Deleted the option and its remap block. `ZEROMOE_LLAMA_CPP_ROOT` stays as a deprecated alias for `ZEROMOE_GGML_ROOT`. |

Stale `-DZEROMOE_USE_GGML=ON` / `-DZEROMOE_BUILD_SERVER=ON` arguments were also
removed from `setup.sh`, `scripts/build_with_ggml.sh`, `bench/build_native.sh`,
`tests/run_components.sh`, the build how-tos and the measurement notes, so
following a documented command no longer produces an unused-variable warning.

Verification for this batch:

- Fresh CPU configure (`-DZEROMOE_CPU_ONLY=ON -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release`) and
  build of `zeromoe_cpu`, `server_tests`, `cpu_api_tests` and the graph-settings
  tests; full `ctest` passes.
- `-DZEROMOE_USE_GGML=OFF`, `-DZEROMOE_BUILD_SERVER=OFF` and
  `-DZEROMOE_USE_LLAMA_CPP=ON` are no longer options: they are ignored as
  unused variables instead of silently selecting an unsupported build.
- `python3 tools/config_inventory.py --check` passes; the macro inventory drops
  to four names and the CMake option list to nine entries.
- A source/script/doc grep finds no remaining `ZEROMOE_USE_GGML`,
  `ZEROMOE_BUILD_SERVER` or `ZEROMOE_USE_LLAMA_CPP` condition, definition or
  flag.

### 2026-10-27 — CUDA builds always link ggml-cuda

`ZEROMOE_USE_GGML_CUDA` defaulted to `OFF`, but `OFF` was not a working
configuration. `models/graph/full_graph_models.cpp` calls
`qwen4exp_full_graph_bootstrap()`, and that definition only exists inside the
guarded half of `models/qwen/ggml_qwen4exp_graph.cpp`; the `#else` stub list
never provided it. A CUDA configure that left the option off failed at link
time, which the inventory missed because it only inspected preprocessor
conditions. macOS already forces `ZEROMOE_CPU_ONLY=ON`, so "CUDA build" and
"ggml-cuda" are the same set.

| Control | Surface | Evidence | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ZEROMOE_USE_GGML_CUDA` (K0027) | Internal compile definition | It is required to compile CUDA-only translation units and is derived from upstream `GGML_CUDA`. | Kept internal; `GGML_CUDA` is the sole public CUDA build switch. |

Stale `-DZEROMOE_USE_GGML_CUDA=ON` arguments were removed from
`CMakePresets.json` (the `cuda` preset), `scripts/build_with_ggml.sh`,
`bench/build_native.sh`, the README build recipe, `docs/env-vars.md`, the
API-server and measurement notes, and the two revert how-tos. The macro itself
stays as the internal capability that keeps CUDA-only translation units out of
CPU-only builds; it is no longer a configure-time input.

### 2026-10-27 — remaining compile-time macros (current tree)

`tools/config_inventory.py --json` scans `core/`, `memory/`, `models/`,
`backends/` and `kernels/` for `ZEROMOE_*`/`GGML_*` names in preprocessor
conditions. The current tree has **four `ZEROMOE_*` source guards**. All are
build capabilities rather than per-request knobs, and none was removed in this
pass.

| Macro | Defined by | Guard sites | Role | Disposition |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- | --- |
| `ZEROMOE_USE_GGML_CUDA` | Derived in `CMakeLists.txt` from upstream `GGML_CUDA`; compile definition on the engine and tests | 84 | Enables the ggml CUDA backend and the CUDA full-graph code paths. | Internal capability only; no configure-time option, so a CUDA build cannot omit it. |
| `ZEROMOE_EXL3_CUDA` | `CMakeLists.txt` target definitions | 18 | Compiles EXL3-specific CUDA decode/backend sections. | Keep as an internal capability guard: `zeromoe_tests` builds with `ZEROMOE_USE_GGML_CUDA` but intentionally omits this EXL3-only define. It is not a user option to merge into `GGML_CUDA`. |
| `ZEROMOE_EXL3_UPSTREAM` | `CMakeLists.txt` source compile definition | 4 | Internal capability guard set only when `ZEROMOE_EXLLAMAV3_SRC` provides the optional upstream sources. | Keep internal; it is not a standalone user or A/B setting. Runtime cooperative routed-FFN selection is controlled separately. |
| `ZEROMOE_METAL` | `CMakeLists.txt:80`; compile definition at `1241` | 2 | Default ON for Apple Silicon. Dispatches prefill-sized F32 matmul (`S>=32`) to Metal/MPS in the NEON backend. | Keep as a platform capability; decode stays on NEON. |

`ZEROMOE_ARM_NEON`, `ZEROMOE_HAVE_CUFILE`, `ZEROMOE_USE_GGML` and
`ZEROMOE_BUILD_SERVER` are recorded as removed in the log above and no longer
appear in the inventory.

### 2026-10-27 — retire the experimental GLM any-type GPU assist override

### Metal on macOS (default on Apple Silicon)

On macOS, `GGML_CUDA` is forced OFF, so CUDA and ggml-cuda code are not built.
Metal is the only GPU path on macOS:

- `ZEROMOE_METAL` defaults **ON** on Apple Silicon (`CMakeLists.txt` derives
  the default from `APPLE AND CMAKE_SYSTEM_PROCESSOR=arm64`) and warns when
  forced ON on any other platform. It adds
  `backends/cpu/kernels_metal.mm` to `kernels_cpu`, defines `ZEROMOE_METAL=1`
  publicly, and links `Metal` + `MetalPerformanceShaders` (plus `Foundation`
  for the final executables).
- The only caller is the F32 `matmul` in `backends/cpu/kernels_cpu_neon.cpp`
  (`:20` and `:132`). Quantized GEMV/GEMM kernels never dispatch to Metal.
  Dispatch requires at least 32 rows and `S*I*O >= 256K` FLOPs. The row gate
  keeps decode (`S=1`) and small speculative batches on NEON, because each MPS
  call re-uploads the whole weight matrix. Both thresholds are heuristics, not
  a measured crossover; no Apple hardware was available to measure one.
- Each call uploads `x`, `W` and the result through new
  `MTLResourceStorageModeShared` buffers, so the weights are copied to the
  device per call. That amortises over prefill-sized `S` but would be slower
  than NEON for `S=1` decode, which is why the row gate exists.
- ggml's own Metal backend is forced OFF (`GGML_METAL`; K0006, superseding the
  snapshot's "keep capability" recommendation). The CPU-only engine never
  creates a ggml backend or scheduler — it uses ggml only for reference
  dequantization — so `GGML_METAL` would build and link a library no ZeroMoE
  code selects, and would add a full-Xcode shader-compiler requirement. It is
  not a second execution path.
- Coverage: `test_components` includes a 64x256x256 F32 matmul, above both
  thresholds, so a CMake build on Apple Silicon exercises MPS numerics.
  `tests/run_components.sh` now defines `ZEROMOE_METAL=1` on arm64 so the
  standalone subset runner matches the CMake default instead of silently
  testing NEON.
- `tests/test_components.cpp` undefines `NDEBUG` before its includes. The
  default `Release` build compiled every `assert` out, which skipped the
  numeric checks and also aborted the suite in `exl3_ngram_reader` (its
  `symlink()`/`unlink()` calls live inside `assert`). With assertions enabled
  all 46 component tests pass in a Linux CPU-only build.

ggml, the API server and ggml-cuda are no longer configurable: they are always
linked and built when the target platform supports them, so no configure-time
input can request a build the tree does not support. Removing those options
also removed their cache entries, which is why the build-cache list below is
shorter than in earlier snapshots.

Build-time inputs that are not source guards remain, but they configure the
build rather than branch engine code:

- **1 ZeroMoE-specific CMake option**: `ZEROMOE_METAL`.
- **4 ZeroMoE build-cache inputs**: `ZEROMOE_EXLLAMAV3_SRC`, `ZEROMOE_GGML_COMMIT`, `ZEROMOE_GGML_PREBUILT_DIR`, and `ZEROMOE_GGML_ROOT`.
- **9 forwarded `GGML_*` options**: `GGML_AVX512`, `GGML_AVX512_VNNI`,
  `GGML_CUDA`, `GGML_CUDA_GRAPHS`, `GGML_HIPBLAS`, `GGML_METAL`,
  `GGML_OPENMP`, `GGML_SYCL` and `GGML_VULKAN` (K0001-K0009). These belong to
  the ggml dependency and are not ZeroMoE source guards.

The JSON's recommendation rows still carry the pre-merge proposals for
K0017-K0035 and its `counts` object still describes the 2026-10-26 snapshot.
Only the `review_decision` and `removal_status` fields of the rows retired in
the removal log above were updated; this section records the current source
inventory.

### Findings deliberately left in place

No additional build input was removed in this pass without tracing its
consumers. The remaining cache values select source trees, prebuilt libraries,
dependency revisions or optional EXL3 checkout paths.

## How to use

The tables contain one row per **surface/name**, not one independent behavior. The same behavior may have TOML, environment, CLI and graph aliases.
IDs are snapshot-local; track decisions by `surface` + `name` when regenerating after a merge.
Every row includes when it is read, a proposed keep/remove/combine decision, affected feature and change risk.
The companion [JSON](configuration-knob-audit.json) includes source excerpts, declaration comments/initializers, file hashes, all discovered worktree references, member-name occurrence candidates, validation requirements and `review_decision` fields (filled in for reviewed rows).
**Recommendations are source-linked candidates, not individually proven safe deletions.** A lexical occurrence cannot prove a knob obsolete or a runtime branch reachable. Exact enclosing predicates/defaults must be checked before edits.
`when used` means execution phase, not introduction date or observed recent use. No history/benchmark reachability claims are made.

## Agreed direction

- Clean break is acceptable: small public allowlist; no permanent legacy alias layer.
- Normal settings describe model, context, devices, optional resource ceilings and logging; generation/request and serving semantics remain separate.
- Derive architecture/dimensions from GGUF. Correct operators and mandatory graph paths belong to adapters. Non-GGUF formats may require additional metadata.
- Resource budgets, hotset placement, threads, staging and prefetch belong to internal planner/controller state, not hundreds of public switches.
- Keep maintained developer tooling; consolidate observational flags into INFO plus per-file DEBUG/VERBOSE. Keep explicit costly profiling/dumps and behavior-changing experiments separate.
- Build identity is generated metadata. Dependency revisions still need internal reproducible pins; logits hashes are numerical evidence, not source hashes.
- Startup calibration target: <=5 seconds on first launch; cache valid measurements. Runtime adaptation changes bounded policies, not captured tensor pointers.
- Preserve mandatory memory/graph safety and per-frame numerical correctness. The 95% performance target remains unverified.

## Existing work to preserve

The active resolver now implements 38 canonical graph fields with strict TOML validation, generated CLI flags, startup binding and Qwen placement/thread wiring. Legacy environment aliases remain during migration; the final public interface is still intended to be smaller.
That compatibility-first implementation is a transitional baseline, **not** the desired final public interface. The later decision favors a clean break; do not spend more work maintaining hundreds of aliases.
CPU build, settings tests and sanitizer checks passed previously. Linux/CUDA and real-model numerical/performance parity remain unverified.
The API worktree contains native OpenAI/Anthropic serving and opt-in CPU prompt checkpoints; native CUDA state caching is intentionally not claimed safe. Preserve cancellation, backpressure, authentication and state ownership.
MTP work is on its own branch. Do not remove draft/state-related controls as if that branch were already integrated.
Automatic startup calibration, runtime adaptation and file-scoped logging are planned, not implemented by this audit.

| Worktree | Branch | HEAD | Source files |
|---|---|---|---:|
| settings | `refactor/settings-simplification` | `cb785462fa24` | 496 |
| original | `feature/qwen36-cuda-onboarding` | `cb785462fa24` | 495 |
| api | `feat/cpp-api-server` | `cb785462fa24` | 508 |
| mtp | `feature/qwen38-mtp` | `1801c47797d5` | 507 |

All four contain independent local state. Full paths, source digests and dirty-status digests are in the JSON. Rebase the evidence against the integrated checkout before removal.
Excluded: third-party/generated/build outputs, obsolete/experimental directories, dated run archives. Tooling rows are separated from native readers.
Only accessible local worktrees are included; stale Linux worktree registrations are not inspected.
Build-cache rows include `CACHE ... FORCE` assignments that are already internal policy; their recommendations distinguish these from normal user inputs.

### Current native-source inventory (2026-10-29)

Refreshed with `python3 tools/config_inventory.py` against the active checkout
at `e63b96329acc58144262a0fade8eae72c90b810c` on `master`, plus documentation-only worktree changes
changes. The scanner covers `core/`, `memory/`, `models/`, `backends/`,
`kernels/`, root `CMakeLists.txt`, `server/CMakeLists.txt`, and `cmake/*.cmake`.
It expands shared GLM environment aliases into concrete names. It does not scan
`tools/`, `tests/`, `bench/`, shell launchers, third-party code, or other
worktrees. Counts are distinct lexical names, not independent decisions or
proof that a setting is read in a normal run.

| Surface | Names | Notes |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Native environment names | 190 | Exact `ZEROMOE_*` names found by current scanner |
| ZeroMoE CMake build options | 1 | `ZEROMOE_METAL` |
| ZeroMoE CMake cache variables | 4 | Dependency paths/source and revision pins; upstream `GGML_CUDA` is outside this project-prefixed count |
| Compile-time macro names | 7 | Build identity and backend capability guards |
| Registered graph settings | 36 | Typed TOML fields with generated `--graph-*` aliases |
| TOML keys | 232 | Lexical parser calls; not all user tuning knobs |
| Native CLI names | 96 | Includes generated graph flags and benchmark-subcommand flags |

The current inference CLI has no separate test, trace, or timing switch: runtime
profiling follows the common verbosity level, and benchmark controls are scoped
to `zeromoe bench`. `--force-start` remains an explicit operator override for
known-safe disjoint workloads and cgroup-capped regression runs.
The diagnostic graph settings `preflight_strict` and `usage_record_file` remain
because they control fail-closed preflight and ZeroMoE usage-file recording,
not debug output.

The seven compile-time macro names are `ZEROMOE_BUILD_TYPE`,
`ZEROMOE_EXL3_CUDA`, `ZEROMOE_EXL3_UPSTREAM`, `ZEROMOE_GIT_COMMIT`,
`ZEROMOE_METAL`, `ZEROMOE_USE_GGML_CUDA`, and `ZEROMOE_VERSION`. They provide
build identity or compile backend-specific code. `ZEROMOE_ENGINE_CUDA` was a
redundant alias and has been removed; version reporting checks the existing
`ZEROMOE_USE_GGML_CUDA` capability macro.

CUDA selection now uses the upstream `GGML_CUDA` option, matching llama.cpp's
GGML build interface. ZeroMoE seeds its default ON outside macOS and OFF on
macOS, preserving prior behavior; setup selects OFF when CUDA tooling is absent.
`ZEROMOE_USE_GGML_CUDA` remains an internal derived compile capability, not a
second user-facing build option. The retired `ZEROMOE_CPU_ONLY` CMake option
is no longer read by active build files or launch/build documentation.
Host-node GGML patches are now mandatory for source builds. A required patch
failure stops configuration; prebuilt libraries must already be built from the
patched source. Qwen runtime host-node execution remains independently opt-in.

A follow-up source/config sweep found no remaining runtime fields explicitly
marked `DEPRECATED NO-OPS` after retiring the two Qwen4Exp keys. The maintained
`configs/`, `bench/`, and survey TOMLs also have no explicit child values under
the checked disabled parents (`attn_score_gpu`, `dsv4_score_gpu`, decode
prewarm, Qwen router/offload/GPU-stage, RAM promotion/adaptive/gate, move-on-
promotion, `use_active_layer_area`, `prefill_retention_weight`, and prefill-window cap).

A TOML dependency scan also found no active config conflicts among the RAM-to-VRAM
promotion modes, no `disk_batch_padding` profile without `io_uring`, no
`moe_prefill_stream` profile without a grouped GPU-pre-fill mode, and no
speculative active-layer promotion profile with the active-layer area disabled.

A lexical review of the current 213 environment names for `DEBUG`, `DIAG`,
`TRACE`, `TIMING`, `PROFILE`, `PROBE`, `TEST`, `DUMP`, `HISTOGRAM`,
`ROUTE_TAP`, `VERIFY`, and `PILOT` terms identifies 43 names for review. This is
a discovery filter, not a deletion list: it mixes output-only diagnostics with
active routing, prefetch, staging, and residency controls. The source-linked
per-name rows below remain the evidence for each decision.

The JSON companion retains the broader four-worktree inventory generated on
2026-10-26. Its source excerpts and removed-setting rows are historical
snapshot evidence. `review_decision` and `removal_status` record later removal
decisions; those rows do not represent current runtime reads. The rejected
Qwen partial-graph selectors are absent from the current native inventory.
The six-pair performance result and removal rationale remain as history.

Removed Qwen CPU-offload `force_cold_count` and `force_expert` injection
settings; they existed only to force selected routes cold for tests/A-B runs
and had no maintained recipe users. Normal CPU-offload eligibility continues
to come from the usage-ranked Option B mask.

Removed `qwen4exp_decode_gpu_router`, a Qwen-only decode routing selector.
It was false in every maintained recipe; the measured RTX 3090 A/B was 2.93
tok/s with GPU routing versus 5.03/4.97 tok/s with the host router. Batched
prefill GPU routing remains available, while decode uses the measured host
router. Device expert and HC handoff remains independent of routing.

Removed `moe_decode_batched`, a prototype that forced all RAM-resident routed
experts onto CPU during decode. It had no maintained recipe users; its Qwen
A/B measured 5.03/4.97 tok/s on the baseline versus 2.93 tok/s enabled
(about 41% slower). Normal GPU dispatch and its existing CPU fallback remain.

Removed `cpu_dot_ggml_avx2`, the default-off IQ2_XXS/IQ3_XXS CPU-fallback
selector. Isolated calls were 1.7–1.9x faster, but the recorded decode A/B
was 0.81→0.82 tok/s versus a 0.86 baseline, and the IQ3 prototype had a 1.73%
worst-row relative-error caveat. The production path now uses the existing FMA
kernels; direct dot-prototype comparisons remain in the developer test source.

Removed `prefill_dispatch_lookahead_tokens`, a default-off prompt-prefetch
widening control with no maintained recipe users. The measured 8-token-ahead
variant regressed a 237-token prompt from 225.28 s / 1.05 tok/s to 260.23 s /
0.91 tok/s, increased disk traffic, and worsened blocking Phase B time. Removed
the widening branch, per-slot lookahead tag, used/wasted counters, and report;
ordinary current-token RAM loads and the existing pilot prefetch remain.

Removed `prefill_opportunistic_promote`: four measured ON runs produced
0.56 tok/s versus 0.60 tok/s OFF, while prefetch-hint pushes fell from about
930 to 420 because promotion consumed the same free-VRAM slots. Removed its
TOML control, branch, pending-promotion tracking, counters, and false recipe
entries. The core `prefill_warm_via_prefetcher` path and synchronous fallback
remain.

Removed `ram_preload_seed_hit_count`: the optional historical-hit seeding was
unverified on hardware, defaulted off, and had no accepted benchmark result.
Standard pin-file ranking and RAM preload remain unchanged.

Request-stats JSONL (`--stats-file`) is also restricted to `zeromoe bench`;
its only maintained CLI caller is the benchmark/report pipeline.

The developer benchmark suite is now under `zeromoe bench`; `--bench` is no
longer a top-level inference option. `bench/perf_report.py` and maintained
benchmark examples use the subcommand, following llama.cpp's separate
`llama-bench` surface.

The current llama.cpp CLI documents `-v`, `--verbose`, and `--log-verbose`
for all log messages, and `-lv`, `--verbosity`, and `--log-verbosity N` for
numeric thresholds. ZeroMoE now accepts the same spellings; `--verbose` maps
to its all-message VERBOSE threshold. See the upstream
[CLI option reference](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/tools/cli/README.md#L359-L378).

MiMo alias-to-model verification is now unconditional; removed the default-on
`--graph-verify-alias` opt-out because skipping this correctness check could
allow mismatched VRAM expert aliases into execution.

The MiMo-only `--graph-node-trace` level selector was removed; its node,
route and non-finite reports now follow common DEBUG verbosity (`--verbosity 5`).

Both CLI overrides for usage-file writes were removed. The TOML
`freeze_usage_stats` key remains the single control; all maintained survey
profiles explicitly set it to `false` when they need to accumulate usage.

The full-graph gate environment variables removed in the first removal batch
remain only in historical benchmark and handoff records, not live runtime
inputs. Remaining graph environment aliases include Qwen preload/thread and
GLM shared-graph families. These are compatibility aliases; migrate maintained
launchers to canonical TOML/CLI before removing them.

Build settings are a separate surface from runtime environment variables. The
current normal CMake options select build capability or platform policy; the
GGML revision/root/prebuilt-directory and EXL3 source cache values identify
dependencies or local build inputs. Keep those identities reproducible, and
prefer CPU/CUDA presets for normal builds. Provenance stamping is mandatory
for the CUDA engine binary rather than a user-selectable option.

### llama.cpp reference scan (2026-10-28)

Reference: the local upstream checkout at commit `8baabd53b77d`, reading
`tools/cli/README.md`, `tools/server/README.md`, `docs/speculative.md`,
and `docs/multi-gpu.md`. The `/Users/rayxu/github/llama.cpp` path from the
request is not mounted in this Linux workspace; this checkout is at the same
pinned revision used by ZeroMoE's GGML integration.

Common CLI names now match the reference for model, prompt, context size,
prediction length, CPU threads, temperature, top-p, main GPU, log file,
verbosity, server alias/API key, and speculative MTP (`spec_type`,
`spec_draft_model`, `spec_draft_n_max`). ZeroMoE does not expose unsupported
llama.cpp flags such as top-k/min-p or speculative algorithms it does not
implement.

The following controls are intentionally ZeroMoE-specific and remain visible
for review because they govern the system's expert-residency and state paths:

| Capability | ZeroMoE settings | Closest llama.cpp reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Expert placement and hot cache | `graph_device_expert_layers`, per-GPU `moe_cache_gb` | `--n-cpu-moe`, `--moe-cache-mib`; similar goal, different resident-tier model |
| Host expert offload and pinning | `graph_offload_*`, `host_ram_gb`, pin budgets | No direct host DDR expert-store or pinned-arena equivalent |
| Route-frequency pin files | `pin_file`, `prefill_pin_file`, `graph_offload_usage_file`, `graph_usage_record_file` | No direct equivalent; controls production/consumption of ZeroMoE's expert-use profiles |
| Multi-GPU expert topology | `[[gpus]]` roles, cache sizes and reservations | `--device`, `--split-mode`, `--tensor-split`; recipes are richer because they assign expert-cache roles |
| Graph-native Qwen MTP | `spec_type`, `spec_draft_*`, `mtp_graph` | `--spec-type draft-mtp`, `--spec-draft-model`, `--spec-draft-n-max`; `mtp_graph` selects ZeroMoE's graph implementation |

These are documented review items, not an exemption for one-off A/B flags.
Unvalidated tuning switches that do not protect a required resource or execution
path should be removed or folded into the planner's default.

Upstream's current CLI and server references were rechecked on 2026-10-28:
they document matching public controls for speculative decoding, API keys,
MoE CPU placement/cache, and multi-GPU device/split topology. Keep familiar
ZeroMoE names aligned where semantics match; do not rename project identity or
ZeroMoE-only settings to `LLAMA_*`. References: [CLI](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/tools/cli/README.md),
[server](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/tools/server/README.md),
[speculative decoding](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/docs/speculative.md),
and [multi-GPU](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/docs/multi-gpu.md).

### ZeroMoE-specific settings retained for review

These controls have no direct llama.cpp equivalent and govern ZeroMoE's
expert placement, usage files, offload, and prefill/prompt behavior. Per the
user's instruction, keep these controls documented for review; do not remove
them solely because llama.cpp lacks an equivalent. Test profiles demonstrate
use, not approval to promote an unvalidated default.

| Capability | Settings | Evidence and disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pin/usage file production and consumption | `qwen4exp_track_router_usage`, `freeze_usage_stats`, `usage_record_file`, `pin_file`, `prefill_pin_file`, `graph_offload_usage_file`, `graph_usage_record_file`, `pin_topk_per_layer`, `pin_threshold` | These create, freeze, rank, and consume expert-use data for preload and placement. Keep visible for review. |
| Option B global hot pool | `qwen4exp_option_b_global_top_k`, `qwen4exp_option_b_global_floor_per_layer`, `qwen4exp_option_b_selected_layers` | **Validated and enabled** in `configs/qwen38-flash.toml`: K=14,000 across all 48 layers, minimum one hot expert per layer. The 7-pair A/B measured 31.49 tok/s for the global 48-layer pool vs 31.00 for the right-sized 44-layer pool (+1.6%, 7/7 wins). A later K=14,554 run measured 43.12 tok/s on its 200-token decode workload. K remains hardware- and usage-file-specific; keep the tuning fields. See `docs/option-b-vram-budget-2026-10-05.md` and `docs/perf-baselines.md`.  The Qwen recipe omits `qwen4exp_option_b_min_coverage` and `qwen4exp_option_b_right_size_top_n` in this mode because they are ignored when the global pool is active.  The recipe also omits redundant default sentinels for per-layer selection, top-N ceiling, and disabled staging; it keeps the validated all-layer/K/floor settings.  Split-benchmark and non-Option-B survey profiles also inherit inactive Option-B defaults; the layer-13 survey keeps only its active Option-B parameters. |
| Qwen CPU/DDR offload and alternate Option B layouts | `qwen4exp_cpu_offload`, `qwen4exp_cpu_offload_layers`, `qwen4exp_option_b_*` (per-layer/concentrated/staging variants), `qwen4exp_cpu_offload_ring_slots`, `qwen4exp_prefill_state_handoff`, `qwen4exp_cpu_offload_event_fence`, `qwen4exp_decode_batch_stage`, `qwen4exp_decode_scheduler` | These select whole CPU-offloaded layers, RAM-to-VRAM staging, or the scheduler-split decode prototype. Keep visible for user review as ZeroMoE offload/scheduling paths. The event-fence and scheduler paths remain off by default; no accepted end-to-end throughput result was found for either. Removed force-cold/force-expert injectors are retired. |
| Qwen GPU staging and router pilot | `qwen4exp_gpu_stage_enabled`, `qwen4exp_gpu_stage_layers`, `qwen4exp_router_pilot`, `qwen4exp_router_pilot_*` | GPU staging is exercised by a dedicated survey profile; router prediction/prefetch is survey-only and off by default. Keep for review as ZeroMoE-specific placement and prefetch behavior. |
| Widened prefill router prediction and hint push | `pilot_widen_all_tokens`, `prefetch_widen_all_tokens` | Prediction has real-hardware coverage/recall measurements at a 16-token prompt, but the extra router work has not been evaluated at representative prompt lengths. The dependent disk-hint push has no real-hardware benefit result and requires prediction to be enabled. Keep both documented and off by default for user review; do not internalize until a measured default or replacement policy is accepted. |
| RAM/VRAM residency and promotion | `ram_cache_layer_adaptive`, `ram_cache_total_experts`, `ram_copy_reclaim_threshold`, `ram_pin_eager_preregister`, `ram_vram_promote_*`, `graph_dynamic_cache` | These control cache capacity, replacement, and expert promotion. Some variants have negative or incomplete A/B evidence; retain for review and preserve the optimized defaults. |
| Expert transfer and prefetch | `decode_async_prewarm`, `decode_async_prewarm_max_batch`, `decode_async_prewarm_nonblocking`, `prefill_warm_via_prefetcher`, `prefetch_pilot_*`, `prefetch_attn_pilot*`, `disk_batch_padding*`, `expert_fetch_threads`, `prefetch_worker_threads` | These affect ZeroMoE's DDR/RAM/VRAM transfer schedule. Keep controls visible; retain measured defaults and keep unvalidated experiments off. |
| Model-specific GPU paths and scheduling | `dsv4_score_gpu`, `dsv4_score_gpu_max_vram_gb`, `qwen4exp_hc_async_readback`, `attn_score_threads` | DSV4 GPU scoring has a correctness microbenchmark but no real-hardware engine result; HC readback is off by default; attention score thread counts are used by maintained GLM/MiMo/DeepSeek recipes. Keep for user review. |
| Survey-only GPU copy-stream switch | `dense_async_copies` | It is enabled only in `tests/moe_usage_survey/survey_config_qwen4exp.toml`, not the shipped Qwen profile; default remains off and no controlled end-to-end result supports promotion. Keep for review as an event-ordered transfer path; the existing environment name remains unchanged. |

## Manually checked exceptions

| Control | What source confirms | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| `ZEROMOE_THREAD_PROFILE`, `_RT`, `_APPLY_NICE` | Removed 2026-10-28. The only consumers were opt-in thread affinity/priority hooks; normal scheduler behavior was a no-op without them. | Removed the developer-only affinity/RT/nice hooks and their call sites. |
| `ZEROMOE_DECODE_LOGITS_HASH` | Decode logits fingerprint is computed and emitted at DEBUG verbosity (`[TEACHER-FORCE-LOGITS]`); the dedicated env gate was removed 2026-10-28. |
| `ZEROMOE_GGML_COMMIT` | `CMakeLists.txt`: selects revision fetched when local ggml is unavailable. | Internal reproducible dependency pin; do not delete dependency identity. |
| `ZEROMOE_LLAMA_CPP_TAG` | CMake references only emit a warning that it is no longer used. | Remove the warning-only compatibility input; keep the real dependency pin. |
| `ZEROMOE_API_KEY` | API `backends/cpu/cpu_main.cpp:93-96`: secret read and non-loopback authentication requirement. | Preserve authentication input; never log its value. |
| `model family` | `models/base/model_family.hpp`: detection already uses GGUF architecture and tensor metadata. | No new family-selection knob; preserve loader/adapter validation. |
| `trace_filter`, `trace_abort_on_nan` | Both settings were removed 2026-10-28; `trace_filter` has no parser/reader, and `check_finite` logs NaN/Inf but does not abort. Tensor trace tags remain available under VERBOSE for log post-filtering. | Removed redundant diagnostics controls; no environment aliases remain. |

## Risk and action semantics

| Risk | Meaning |
|---|---|
| LOW | Primarily help/output/internal bookkeeping; still check parsing and consumers. |
| MEDIUM | Can break request semantics, scripts, observability or workload performance. |
| HIGH | May affect model numerics, state, graph bindings, asynchronous memory lifetime, OOM safety, platform builds or major performance policy. |

**INTERNALIZE** means remove the external selector, retain its required behavior in typed engine state, and assign an owner. It does not mean delete the algorithm.
**COMBINE** means preserve independent semantics while unifying input/diagnostics; verbosity must not activate execution experiments.
**KEEP DEV/TOOL** is conditional on a maintained use; it is not a requirement to keep old names.
No row is automatically safe to delete based on risk alone. Every removal is recorded in the log with its evidence; no additional engine runtime flag is declared dead from lexical counts alone.

## Removal batches

1. Integrate/rebase worktrees and define the public allowlist; keep dirty baselines intact.
2. Consolidate scoped observational logging/help. Keep dumps, synchronization checks and numerical validation separate.
3. Internalize adapter/backend correctness and derive GGUF facts. Preserve measured recipes as internal policy tests, not hundreds of required settings.
4. Replace memory/thread/prefetch/placement inputs with startup planner outputs; retain explicit resource ceilings and mandatory reserves.
5. Introduce conservative runtime policy adaptation within preallocated resources, with cooldowns/backoff and safe concurrency.
6. Remove superseded parsers, aliases and unused experiments in coherent batches; migrate maintained scripts. Unknown removed input must fail explicitly.

Validate each behavioral batch with per-decode-frame teacher forcing, applicable kernel checks, graph full-fit conformance, memory-pressure cases and phase-separated performance. Do not combine all deletions and tuning changes into one unreviewable patch.

## Dynamic lookup follow-up

Indirect `getenv(name)`/registry lookups and family namespace prefixes are not independent knobs. The JSON lists all discovered locations under `dynamic_lookups`; expanding each constructed name requires tracing its caller/registry.
The typed graph aliases are linked to their registered canonical fields where present. Other dynamically assembled names may not be fully enumerated.

## Resume checklist

- Start from the integrated revision; compare the JSON snapshot/file hashes before trusting line numbers.
- Choose an ownership batch, not a random list of low-risk names.
- Fill `review_decision`, identify maintained consumers and replacement state, then remove parser/wiring/scripts together.
- Do not copy experimental values or defaults across model families without checking equations/capabilities.
- Record actual external controls removed, internalized and combined separately; do not count renamed aliases as deletions.
- Linux/CUDA plus real weights are required before claiming graph correctness/performance parity.

### 2026-10-29 — Ling batched negative-id override

Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_BATCHED_NEGATIVE_SKIP` and the runner's A/B override. Ling now keeps valid dummy ids for MMQ graph batches of 64 tokens or fewer, preserving the measured two-token prefill behavior, and uses patched negative-id skipping for batches above 64 to avoid GGML row-compaction overruns. Non-Ling MMQ behavior is unchanged. Historical benchmark results remain.

Source inventory, audit JSON, and diff checks pass. Build/runtime validation was not run, per the current instruction to avoid build/CMake work.

### 2026-10-29 — unified CPU thread control

Removed the model-specific `ZEROMOE_*_CPU_THREADS` environment variables, `ZEROMOE_QWEN_HOST_NODE_THREADS`, and the graph-specific `graph_cpu_threads_decode` setting. The shared `-t/--threads` option and `omp_max_threads` config key now control graph CPU backends across models. Existing per-backend fallback counts remain when the shared option is unset. Active benchmark callers were migrated to `--threads`; dated result records remain historical.

Source inventory and diff checks pass. Build/runtime validation was not run, per the current instruction to avoid build/CMake work.

### 2026-10-29 — GLM GPU1 hot-set policy

Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_GPU1_HOT_ON_GPU0`, the one-off placement verification override. Hot expert buffers now always allocate on GPU1. Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_GPU1_HOT_PRELOAD`; startup always attempts the measured preload and preserves the warning plus first-decode upload fallback on failure. Historical correctness and performance records remain.

Source inventory and diff checks pass. Build/runtime validation was not run, per the current instruction to avoid build/CMake work.

### 2026-10-29 — GLM prefill branch summation

Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_PIN_BRANCH_SUM`. The measured policy pins the routed experts' prefill sum to the GPU that ran the branch, sending the 13 MiB summed tensor instead of the 105 MiB unreduced tensor across devices. Historical A/B results remain.

Source inventory and diff checks pass. CUDA build and graph-settings tests will verify the change; no model benchmark was run.

### 2026-10-29 — GLM pilot experiment controls

Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_PILOT_LOOKAHEAD`, `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_PILOT_MIN_SCORE`, and `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_PILOT_RIDGE`. The pilot uses depth one while staging and measured depth two in prefetch-only mode, has no score cutoff, and uses the plain next-layer router scores. `PILOT_STAGE` and `PILOT_PREFETCH` remain. Historical analysis records remain unchanged.

Source inventory and diff checks pass. CUDA build and graph-settings tests are required before considering this batch verified; model runtime benchmarks were not run.

### 2026-10-29 — GLM prefill ring geometry

Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_PREFILL_PREFETCH_CHUNK` and `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_PREFILL_PREFETCH_SETS`. The ring now uses two-layer transfer chunks and two reusable sets, matching the measured shipped policy. The separate delay, drain, and early-start controls remain because they select I/O scheduling behavior.

Source inventory and diff checks pass. CUDA build and graph-settings tests will verify the change; no model benchmark was run.

### 2026-10-29 — GLM prefill scheduler switches

Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_PREFILL_SCHED_EVENTS` and its internal `ZEROMOE_SCHED_EVENTS` `setenv` shim after confirming the pinned GGML source has no reader. Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_PREFILL_SCHED_PARALLEL` and fixed scheduler parallel mode off; parallel input mode multiplies graph inputs and needs roughly 56 GiB of extra workspace.

The CUDA build and graph-settings tests pass. No runtime benchmark was run.

### 2026-10-29 — GLM dynamic heat experiment

Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_DYNAMIC_HEAT` and `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_DYNAMIC_HEAT_HALFLIFE`, along with the heat state and candidate-scoring branch. The default miss-count promotion policy remains. The optional threshold had no maintained caller or validated deployment recipe.

Source inventory and diff checks pass. CUDA build and graph-settings tests will verify the change; no model benchmark was run.

### 2026-10-29 — GLM prefill read-ahead threshold

Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_PREFILL_AHEAD_MIN_TOKENS`. Read-ahead uses the measured 64-token minimum, avoiding excess SSD reads for small prompts. The separate `PREFILL_AHEAD` depth control remains.

Source inventory and diff checks pass. CUDA build and graph-settings tests will verify the change; no model benchmark was run.

### 2026-10-29 — GLM early state export

Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_EARLY_STATE_EXPORT`. Early state/history expansion is now unconditional, matching the measured path that reduced the S=128 live-set peak from 754 MiB to 64 MiB and moved the 805-token prefill from 5.7 to 21.1 tok/s with the same output start. The old `=0` result remains in the dated benchmark record.

Source inventory and diff checks pass. CUDA build and graph-settings tests will verify the change; no runtime benchmark was run.

### 2026-10-29 — GLM prefetch scheduling defaults

Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_PREFILL_PREFETCH_DRAIN` and `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_PREFILL_PREFETCH_EARLY`. Prefetch now always drains each two-layer chunk, allowing small graph-input copies between weight-copy batches, and starts the first eligible layer immediately after ring construction. `PREFILL_PREFETCH` and read-ahead depth remain exposed.

Source inventory and diff checks pass. CUDA build and graph-settings tests will verify the changes; no model benchmark was run.

### 2026-10-29 — second-device assist reserve

Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_ASSIST2_KEEP`. The slower second-device copy path now always leaves one ready expert for CPU execution, matching the measured default. The assist device and slot controls remain.

Source inventory and diff checks pass. CUDA build and graph-settings tests will verify the change; no model benchmark was run.

### 2026-10-29 — GLM/Ling whole-layer placement

Removed `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_GRAPH_PRELOAD_LAYERS` and extended the existing typed `graph_device_expert_layers` setting to GLM/Ling. The capability remains available through the same TOML/CLI key; GLM/Ling default to zero, preserving shared-cache/global-pool placement.

Source inventory and diff checks pass. CUDA build and graph-settings tests will verify the migration; no model benchmark was run.

### 2026-10-29 — GGML MoE A/A dump

Removed `ZEROMOE_GGML_MOE_DUMP` and its one-off layer-0 tensor export/synchronize path. A repository scan found no maintained caller; other numerical and routing diagnostics remain.

Source inventory and diff checks pass. CUDA build and graph-settings tests will verify this deletion; no model benchmark was run.

### 2026-10-29 — benchmark EOS option

Removed `ZEROMOE_BENCH_IGNORE_STOP` and moved maintained benchmark callers to llama.cpp-compatible `--ignore-eos`. Benchmark mode sets the same engine flag internally; regular inference continues stopping at EOS/stop tokens by default.

Source inventory, CLI build, and focused graph-settings tests pass. No model benchmark was run.

### 2026-10-30 — teacher-forced sampling switch

Removed `ZEROMOE_TEACHER_FORCE_SKIP_SAMPLING`. Every maintained caller set it to `1`; when a teacher token exists, generation now uses that token directly and skips sampler invocation. When the teacher token stream ends, ordinary sampling continues. Updated benchmark/test harness references and current usage guidance. Historical run records retain the old variable as provenance.


### 2026-10-30 — Q2_0 VNNI override

Removed `ZEROMOE_Q2_0_DISABLE_VNNI` and its forced GGML fallback branch. Runtime dispatch now uses AVX-512 VNNI when supported and selects the GGML CPU dot on unsupported CPUs. The older controlled comparison found VNNI 1.54x faster with correctness passing. A 2026-10-30 focused smoke test on this worktree had exact output but measured VNNI at 1.71-1.79 ms/pass versus GGML at 0.62-0.71 ms/pass; this unpaired result conflicts with the historical run and needs a controlled matched rerun before making a performance claim. The historical reproduction command is retained and marked obsolete.


### 2026-10-30 — prompt-token logging CLI

Retired the `ZEROMOE_DUMP_PROMPT_TOKENS` environment alias and kept its token-ID capture as `--log-prompts-dir PATH`, matching llama.cpp CLI naming. Each request writes one token-ID file in the requested directory. `ZEROMOE_PROMPT_TOKEN_INPUT_FILE` remains available for explicit replay.


### 2026-10-30 — BF16 expert GEMM env alias

Removed `ZEROMOE_EXPERT_BF16_GEMM`. It only duplicated the `moe_expert_bf16_gemm` TOML control, which remains available for explicit comparisons and defaults off because it can change token-level results. No maintained script depended on the environment alias.

Removed `ZEROMOE_GGML_MUL_MAT_ID`. The existing `moe_ggml_mul_mat_id` TOML key remains the canonical control and is present in maintained Qwen, MiMo, and DeepSeek recipes; no maintained caller used the environment alias.

Removed `ZEROMOE_GGML_SHARED_EXPERT`. The existing `moe_ggml_shared_expert` TOML key remains canonical; active recipes keep their values, and no maintained script used the environment alias.

Retired `ZEROMOE_DEEPSEEK_V41_TRACE_DIR`, `_TRACE_LAYER`, and `_TRACE_POS`. The profiling tool already exposes layer/position filters as CLI options; it now forwards `--trace-dir`, `--trace-layer`, and `--trace-pos` directly to ZeroMoE. The trace output remains available for operation-boundary comparisons, with no environment aliases.


### 2026-10-30 — DeepSeek prefill chunk alias

Removed `ZEROMOE_DEEPSEEK_V41_PREFILL_CHUNK`. DeepSeek now uses the shared `prefill_batch_tokens` field: the default 0 preserves whole-prompt graph prefill, while TOML or llama.cpp-compatible `-ub/--ubatch-size` sets an explicit batch cap. This retires the old 128-token A/B cap that could send the rest of a long prompt through serial decode. No DeepSeek model run was performed.

Removed `ZEROMOE_KQUANT_LEGACY`. The CUDA dispatcher now selects lane8 automatically when the input width satisfies its block/alignment requirements and keeps the cooperative kernel for other shapes. The old variable only forced the comparison path; no maintained caller remains. Historical same-binary A/B notes are retained as evidence.

Removed dead assignments and comments left after earlier removals: the Qwen full-graph environment assignments in `tools/check_adaptive_placement_accuracy.py` (the gate now pins the TOML full-graph keys) and `tools/bench_qwen4exp_mtp_suite.py`; the `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_PILOT_LOOKAHEAD` assignment in `bench/glm53/run_ab.sh`; the retired Qwen thread/early-ready exports and unsets in `configs/qwen4exp_q2_0_option_b_scheduler.env`; and the host-node pin comment in `models/qwen/ggml_qwen4exp_graph_settings.cpp`. Also corrected 74 audit rows whose `removal_status` still said `NOT STARTED` although their `review_decision` and current inventory already recorded the removal.

### 2026-10-30 — Promo and MTP controls become typed Qwen settings
| Control | Surface | Evidence | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ZEROMOE_PROMO_CAP`, `_BURST`, `_MIN_MISSES`, `_FREQ_WEIGHT`, `_DECAY_EVERY` | environment | `PromotionConfig` already carried these as typed constants; the env reads were the only runtime override. | Added `graph_promo_cap`, `graph_promo_burst`, `graph_promo_min_misses`, `graph_promo_freq_weight`, `graph_promo_decay_every` to the shared graph settings, claimed by Qwen only, with defaults 12/20/2/4096/16000000. `promotion_init` now reads `active_settings().tun`. |
| `ZEROMOE_MTP_PREFILL_OVERLAP` | environment | The reader only checked `=1`; the guard already decides whether overlap engages. | Added `graph_mtp_prefill_overlap` (default 0) and read it in `qwen4exp_graph_mtp_prefill_begin`. `tools/bench_qwen4exp_mtp_suite.py` now passes `--graph-mtp-prefill-overlap`. |
| `ZEROMOE_MTP_GREEDY_FAST_PATH` | environment | The fast path is the safe default at temperature 0 and has no measured downside. | Removed the env switch; the direct argmax path is always used when `temperature <= 0`. |
| `ZEROMOE_QWEN4EXP_EXPERT_PROMOTION` | environment alias | TOML `expert_promotion` is the canonical control and is already resolved in `EngineRunner`. | Removed the environment override; `config.expert_promotion` is authoritative. |
| `ZEROMOE_QWEN4EXP_ROUTE_IDS_DUMP` | environment | It was an optional route-ID capture with no runtime effect. | Removed the capture and its file handle; existing dumps remain usable offline. |
| `ZEROMOE_TEST_GLM53_DIR` | test environment | Fixture path is test-only and was the last `ZEROMOE_TEST_*` env used by the GLM native tests. | Migrated to `zeromoe_tests --glm53-dir <path>` (also `--glm53-dir=<path>`). |
| `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_PREFILL_CPU_THREADS`, `ZEROMOE_GLM5NEXT_CPU_THREADS` | environment | GLM now uses the shared process-wide `--threads` option; prefill is capped at `min(threads, 6)`. | `bench/glm_gsqrco_pool.sh` now passes `--threads` and no longer exports the removed names. |

Inventory: environment 120 -> 111, CLI 101 -> 107, graph settings 35 -> 41, test environment 18 -> 17. Rebuilt CUDA `zeromoe`; provenance check pending commit.
